# PAN-5504: accuracy audit of the Guides and Reference docs groups

**Issue:** https://github.com/eltmon/overdeck/issues/5504 (part of epic #5467, docs overhaul)
**Priority:** P1. Docs-only change. No code changes, no `changelog.d` entry.
**Base:** `main` at `574a6be2c29` (v0.89.1 plus later merges), 2026-11-19. Both prerequisite PRs named in the issue
have merged, and their commits are in the history of the page they edited: PAN-5472 (#5535, `94f8a78502d`) on
`reference/god-view.mdx`, and PAN-5482 (#5558, `0558b4ab739`) on `reference/template-conversations.mdx`. No open PR
touches `guides/*.mdx` or `reference/*.mdx` (checked with `gh pr list --state open --json files`). So this audit works
on top of both.

## Summary

The issue asks for a line-by-line accuracy audit of every published page in the **Guides** and **Reference** navigation
groups. The planning session already did the audit. Eight research passes read all 18 pages (4,709 lines) against the
code on the base. The planner then checked the change tables by script: each grep anchor matches its page exactly once,
and each evidence quote written as `path:line "quote"` greps in its file (about 670 quotes; citations written as a
command and its result, or as a bare description, were not machine-checked). The planner re-ran the evidence by hand for
every section deletion and every "no match" claim that a row depends on:

- the deleted stale-check re-trigger service (`src/dashboard/server/main.ts:300` "PAN-3917: the merge-blocker,
  review-status, and stale-check reconcilers are gone");
- the corrupted-workspace dashboard UI (`git grep -i 'Workspace Corrupted\|Clean & Recreate' -- src/dashboard/frontend/src`
  prints nothing);
- no prompt cache (`git grep clearPromptCache -- src` prints nothing), eleven templates in `src/lib/cloister/prompts/`
  including `uat-agent.md` and `work-deft.md`, and exactly four production `renderPrompt(` callers;
- the old Deacon loop in `src/lib/health.ts`, reached only from `pan show <id> --health` (`src/cli/commands/show.ts:17-18`);
- `.planning/` survives only as a legacy check in `src/cli/commands/release.ts:441`;
- no "frozen after done" lane rule (`git grep -i frozen -- src/lib/lanes src/cli/commands/lane.ts` prints nothing);
- no code creates the `state-final` tag (`git grep state-final -- src scripts` prints nothing);
- `.claude/skills/` is in Overdeck's project gitignore entries (`src/lib/workspace-sync-paths.ts:18`);
- `pan restart` and `pan reload` treat auto-resume differently (`src/cli/commands/restart.ts:257`, `src/cli/commands/reload.ts:463`);
- the token report figures (76,462 calls, $15,552, data through 2026-07-06, "deliberately not imported") all appear in
  `docs/token-spend-report/index.html`;
- the omp MCP claim (see Decision D10, which the planner narrowed after this check). This PRD lists every wrong, stale or removed
claim per page, with the exact current text, the replacement text and the code evidence (Appendices A to H).

The implementation agent applies those changes, removes every en and em dash, fixes frontmatter, fixes the one broken
anchor, checks links, runs the Mintlify validators, and writes a PR body with one note per page. It does not need to
re-research. It re-checks a claim only when a grep anchor no longer matches.

## Glossary

- **Guides group**: the `"group": "Guides"` block in `docs.json` (line 175 on the base, under the `Guides` tab). It lists
  five pages: `guides/operating`, `guides/legacy-codebases`, `guides/skill-pack-authoring`, `guides/stitch-integration`,
  `guides/docker-setup`.
- **Reference group**: the `"group": "Reference"` block in `docs.json` (line 190, under the `Reference` tab). It lists
  twelve pages: `reference/architecture`, `reference/settings`, `reference/god-view`, `reference/agents-directory`,
  `reference/workers`, `reference/lanes`, `reference/state-model`, `reference/harness-landscape`,
  `reference/posthog-agents`, `reference/prompts`, `reference/troubleshooting`, `reference/token-spend-report`.
- **Roadmap group**: the `"group": "Roadmap"` block in the same tab. It lists `vision` (audited by #5499, not here) and
  `reference/template-conversations` (listed in this issue, so audited here).
- **Listed pages**: the 16 pages named in the issue body. **Added pages**: `guides/operating` (added by PAN-5482 on
  2026-11-18) and `reference/settings` (added by PAN-5477 on 2026-11-18). See Decision D1.
- **Claim**: any statement on a page a reader could act on: a command, subcommand or flag, a config key or default, a UI
  label or location, a file path, a port, a behavior, a limit, a list of supported harnesses, trackers or platforms, or a
  maturity label.
- **Primary source**: the code on the base (`git grep`), the Commander definitions under `src/cli/`,
  `pan <verb> [sub] --help` (the installed `pan` is 0.89.1 and matches this tree), `src/lib/config-yaml/defaults.ts` and
  `schema.ts`, the dashboard source under `src/dashboard/frontend/src/` and `src/dashboard/server/`, and `changelog.mdx`.
  Other `.mdx` pages, `docs/*.md`, `README.md` and skills are never proof. One exception: when a page describes a
  skill's own content (the Stitch skills, the `token-spend-report` dev skill), that skill file counts.
- **Grep anchor**: the quoted current text in each change table. Line numbers drift as earlier edits land, so find the
  text with `grep -n -F '<anchor>' <page>`, not by line number. An anchor is the text inside the outer backticks of the
  "Grep anchor" cell. Where a cell holds `\|`, the page has a plain `|`. Where a replacement holds `` \` ``, type a plain
  backtick.
- **Dash rule**: no U+2013 (en dash) or U+2014 (em dash) anywhere in a touched page, frontmatter and body. Check:
  `grep -nP '[\x{2013}\x{2014}]' <page>` prints nothing. Replace a dash with a comma, colon, period, parentheses, or the
  word "to" for a range. A dash that stands alone in a table cell to mean "nothing here" becomes `none`. Arrows (`→`) and
  box-drawing characters (`─`, `│`, `├`) are not dashes. A plain ASCII hyphen-minus `-` is not a dash. The change tables
  do not list each dash; the executor removes them mechanically, except where a row gives specific wording.
- **Frontmatter rule**: `title` is specific and searchable. `description` is 50 to 160 characters, counting the text
  between the quotes. Neither contains a dash. A value containing `: ` is double-quoted. When this PRD retitles a page it
  also adds `sidebarTitle: "<the old title>"`, unless the page already has one, so the navigation label stays short.
- **Experimental**: a dashboard view hidden unless Settings → Experimental → **Experimental features** is on. The gated
  tab ids are in `EXPERIMENTAL_TAB_IDS` (`src/dashboard/frontend/src/lib/experimentalFeatures.ts:4`): `agents`,
  `autopreso`, `resources`, `sessions`, `metrics`, `costs`, `health`, `god-view`. Wording at a page's first mention of such
  a view: "(experimental: turn on Settings → Experimental → **Experimental features** to show it)". Later mentions on the
  same page may say "(experimental)".
- **Follow-ups**: code bugs or out-of-scope doc problems found during the audit. They go in a `## Follow-ups` list in the
  PR body. This issue changes no code.
- **PR note**: a one-line summary of what changed on a page, or "verified, no change". Each page's last item puts its note
  in its commit message as a line `PR-note: <path>.mdx: <note>`. The validation item collects them into the PR body.
- **Help sentence**: "Still stuck? Ask on the [Overdeck Discord](https://discord.gg/ZpHE2ZTv5P) or file a
  [GitHub Issue](https://github.com/eltmon/overdeck/issues)."


## Background and evidence

- **Scope grew from 16 to 18 pages.** The issue lists 16 pages. Since it was filed, two pages joined these groups:
  `guides/operating` (PAN-5482, #5558) and `reference/settings` (PAN-5477, #5570). No other audit issue covers them:
  `gh issue view <n> --json body | grep -E 'reference/settings|guides/operating'` prints nothing for #5499, #5500, #5501,
  #5502 and #5503, and none of their PRDs audits either page. The issue's scope sentence is "every published page in the
  Guides and Reference nav group". PAN-5501, PAN-5502 and PAN-5503 made the same call for their groups. Decision D1
  includes them. Both were written on 2026-11-18, so their tables are short.
- **Before-state per page** (dash lines counted with the dash-rule grep; description length without quotes):

| Page | Lines | Dash lines | Title | Description chars | sidebarTitle | Change rows |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| guides/operating | 88 | 0 | Operating Overdeck: stop, restart, recover and back up | 140 | Operating Overdeck | 9 |
| guides/legacy-codebases | 134 | 0 | Legacy Codebase Support | 52 | none | 8 |
| guides/skill-pack-authoring | 205 | 18 | Authoring a Skill Pack | 55 | none | 8 |
| guides/stitch-integration | 188 | 0 | Google Stitch Integration | 143 | none | 14 |
| guides/docker-setup | 352 | 0 | Docker & HTTPS Setup | 134 | none | 21 + V2 (row 22 replaced) |
| reference/architecture | 331 | 7 | Overdeck architecture | 143 | Architecture | 23 |
| reference/settings | 45 | 0 | Settings reference: every section of the Overdeck Settings page | 105 | Settings reference | 11 |
| reference/god-view | 91 | 0 | God View | 114 | none | 4 |
| reference/agents-directory | 151 | 8 | Agents page | 101 | none | 6 |
| reference/workers | 317 | 6 | Delegate to worker agents | 96 | Workers | 15 |
| reference/lanes | 383 | 6 | Gauntlet lanes | 82 | none | 16 |
| reference/state-model | 102 | 0 | State Model | 94 | none | 13 |
| reference/harness-landscape | 666 | 139 | Harness Landscape | 108 | none | 31 |
| reference/posthog-agents | 119 | 0 | PostHog for coding agents | 75 | none | 3 |
| reference/template-conversations | 312 | 36 | Template Conversations | 115 | none | 21 |
| reference/prompts | 631 | 42 | Prompt Templates | 152 | none | 47 |
| reference/troubleshooting | 629 | 14 | Troubleshooting Overdeck | 145 | Troubleshooting | 22 + V1 |
| reference/token-spend-report | 95 | 6 | Token Spend Report | 79 | none | 9 |

  Total: 4,839 lines, 282 dash lines, 282 change rows (plus the two planner rows V1 and V2 below).
- **Row verification.** The planner ran a script over every change table. Each grep anchor matches its page exactly once
  (`grep -c -F '<anchor>' <page>` is 1), and every `path:line "quote"` evidence citation greps in its file. Some anchors
  contain an em dash copied from the page (prompts rows 4, 18, 28, 31; troubleshooting rows 4 and 9). That is why the
  Common procedure applies rows before the dash pass.
- **Validators on the base.** `npx -y mint@4.3.49 broken-links --check-anchors` reports exactly one broken link, and it
  is in this group: `guides/docker-setup.mdx` → `/reference/troubleshooting#slow-vitereact-frontend-with-multiple-workspaces`.
  The target section exists as `## Slow Vite/React Frontend with Multiple Workspaces` (`reference/troubleshooting.mdx:214`).
  Mintlify keeps the `/` in that slug (a research pass confirmed `#slow-vite/react-frontend-with-multiple-workspaces`
  passes the local checker), but a slash in a URL fragment is fragile. Decision D9 renames the heading instead.
- **What breaks the validators** (`.overdeck/context/codebase/concerns.md`, the "`npx mint validate` and
  `npx mint broken-links` pass on `main`" entry): a bare `<word>` in prose outside backticks (MDX reads it as a JSX tag),
  an unquoted frontmatter value containing `: `, an `import ... from "react"` line in a snippet, and relative links to
  files that `.mintignore` excludes. `npm run lint:docs` (`scripts/lint-docs.js`) catches only `$` in prose and `<` before
  a digit. Never run `mint validate --fix`: it rewrites files that sibling issues edit.
- **Tests that read these pages.** Two tests constrain the edits:
  - `src/lib/__tests__/agent-text-cli-verbs.test.ts:55-57` lists `reference/template-conversations.mdx` in
    `PROPOSAL_DOCS` because it "sketches `pan bundle` / `pan template` and says they do not exist yet". The page must keep
    saying both verbs do not exist (`pan --help` lists neither on the base). Run this test after editing the page.
  - `tests/unit/telemetry-docs.test.ts:120-122` requires `reference/posthog-agents` in `docs.json` and the file to exist.
    The page is edited, never deleted or moved.
- **Inbound anchor links** to these pages from other files (`git grep -nE "/(guides|reference)/<page>#"`):
  - `/reference/lanes#lanes-on-other-machines`: `anywhere.mdx:206`, `configuration/linked-machines.mdx:291`, `:648`.
  - `/reference/workers#externally-spawned-agents`: `configuration/harnesses.mdx:1105`, `reference/agents-directory.mdx:129`.
  - `/reference/troubleshooting#wsl2-stability-issues-windows-users`: `guides/docker-setup.mdx:326`.
  - `/reference/troubleshooting#slow-vitereact-frontend-with-multiple-workspaces`: `guides/docker-setup.mdx:345` (broken).
  Those first three headings keep their text (NFR-2). `docs.json` redirects `/configuration/state-branch` to
  `/reference/state-model`; the slug stays.
- **Help sections.** `guides/operating`, `reference/settings` and `reference/troubleshooting` already link the Discord and
  GitHub Issues. Three pages have a troubleshooting section without them: `guides/docker-setup`,
  `guides/stitch-integration` and `reference/prompts`. Their rows add the help sentence (D3).
- **No generator owns these pages.** `git grep` for each page path across `scripts/`, `src/`, `sync-sources/`, `.github/`
  and `tests/` finds only skill pointers (`pan-skill-creator`, `pan-skill-installer`, `pan-lane` skills link the pages) and
  the two tests above. The `token-spend-report` dev skill regenerates `docs/token-spend-report/index.html`, not the
  `.mdx` page. So hand edits are safe and nothing regenerates them.

## Requirements

- **FR-1**: Every wrong, stale or removed claim listed in the work items and appendices is replaced with the listed text
  or deleted as listed. Wording may be adjusted for flow; the fact may not.
- **FR-2**: For any claim on a page that this PRD does not list, the executor applies the residual rule (Common procedure
  step 3). It verifies the claim against a primary source only if the claim looks doubtful. If the code disagrees, it
  fixes the page to match the code. It never changes code.
- **FR-3**: Every page in scope satisfies the dash rule.
- **FR-4**: Every page in scope satisfies the frontmatter rule. The titles and descriptions named in the work items
  change; each retitled page gets a `sidebarTitle` holding the old title (unless it already has one).
- **FR-5**: Every internal link on every page resolves to an existing page, and to an existing heading for an anchor,
  counting `docs.json` redirects. `npx -y mint@4.3.49 broken-links --check-anchors` reports no broken link in a `guides/`
  or `reference/` page, which includes fixing the docker-setup Vite anchor (D9).
- **FR-6**: `guides/docker-setup`, `guides/stitch-integration` and `reference/prompts` end their troubleshooting section
  with the help sentence. `reference/troubleshooting` carries the help sentence under `### Resources`.
  `guides/operating` and `reference/settings` keep both links.
- **FR-7**: Every place a page names an experimental view says it is experimental and names the switch (Glossary,
  "Experimental"). The rows that do this are marked in the appendices' "Labels needed" lists: God View (`reference/god-view`,
  `reference/architecture`), Metrics (`reference/architecture`, `reference/token-spend-report`), Costs
  (`reference/token-spend-report`), AutoPreso (`reference/settings`), the Agents page (`reference/agents-directory`,
  `reference/lanes`, `reference/workers`), Resources (`guides/operating`, `reference/troubleshooting`) and Health (`reference/troubleshooting`).
  `reference/god-view` already carries the label in its opening `<Note>`.
- **FR-8**: Wrong screenshot alt text is rewritten. These `<ThemedImage>` blocks show UI the code no longer renders and
  are deleted: the god-view and metrics images on `reference/architecture` (Appendix E, architecture rows 3 and 13),
  `reference/troubleshooting` Health image (row 19, plus its now-unused import, row 20), and
  `reference/token-spend-report` metrics image (row 5). Image files stay in `images/`.
- **FR-9**: The PR body ticks all 18 pages with a one-line note each. It pastes the clean output of the dash loop,
  `npm run lint:docs`, `npx -y mint@4.3.49 validate` and `npx -y mint@4.3.49 broken-links --check-anchors`. It lists the
  Follow-ups.
- **FR-10**: `reference/template-conversations.mdx` still states that `pan bundle` and `pan template` do not exist, and
  `npx vitest run src/lib/__tests__/agent-text-cli-verbs.test.ts` passes.
- **NFR-1**: No file outside the 18 pages changes, except the PR body. No code, test, rule, image, `docs.json`,
  `CLAUDE.md` or `changelog.d` change. Image files are not deleted even when a page stops using them.
- **NFR-2**: No heading text changes except where a row says so. The rows that change, delete or add a heading are:
  troubleshooting V1 (Vite heading), 4 and 9 (dash in heading); docker-setup 6 (deletes `### Using mkcert` and
  `### For Custom Domains`) and 11 (`### Via Dashboard` → `### With pan up`); stitch 8 (`## STATE.md Integration` →
  `## Recording designs in the PRD draft`); template-conversations 5 (`## Why today's model is not enough` →
  `## Why this was proposed`); harness-landscape 16 (the pi heading) and the dash pass over its `### <name> — <label>`
  headings; architecture 21 (`## PRD Architecture` replaced by block R7); prompts 15 (deletes `### Cache invalidation`),
  27 (adds `### Prompts outside this loader`), 30 and 43; token-spend-report 5 (`## Beyond Cost: Runtime Metrics`
  replaced by block R4); skill-pack-authoring 7 (adds `## MCP servers`); legacy-codebases 7 (adds the block R14 section).
  The dash pass may change any other heading that contains a dash, because no inbound link targets one (checked above).
  These headings keep their exact text: `## Lanes on other machines` (lanes), `## Externally spawned agents` (workers),
  `## WSL2 Stability Issues (Windows Users)` (troubleshooting).
- **NFR-3**: Prose follows the repo writing rules: plain, active, short sentences; no em or en dashes; no hype.

## Decisions

- **D1 (scope)**: audit 18 pages: the 17 pages in the Guides and Reference groups plus `reference/template-conversations`
  (Roadmap group), not only the 16 in the issue body. `vision` (also in the Roadmap group) belongs to #5499. Rationale under
  Background.
- **D2 (prerequisite PRs)**: PAN-5472 and PAN-5482 have merged (Base line). Audit `god-view` and `template-conversations`
  as they are on the base.
- **D3 (help links)**: add the help sentence only where a page talks about getting help or troubleshooting (FR-6). Do not
  add a help section to other pages.
- **D4 (code bugs)**: when the code is wrong rather than the doc, the page states what the code does today and the bug goes
  to Follow-ups. Examples: `pan kill <id>` does not stop a worker on another machine (workers rows 4 and 5); `pan sync
  --backup-only` runs a full sync when sync backups are off (operating). Never describe intended behavior as current.
- **D5 (screenshots)**: this issue adds and replaces no image. Wrong alt text is rewritten. An image that shows UI the code
  no longer renders is removed by deleting its whole `<ThemedImage ... />` block (FR-8), and a recapture goes to
  Follow-ups. An image whose content still matches the code but whose app chrome shows the old "Panopticon v0.21.0" brand
  stays (for example the Costs image on `token-spend-report`); its recapture goes to Follow-ups.
- **D6 (removed features)**: delete the section about a removed feature. Do not keep it with a "removed" note, unless a row
  says to keep a pointer.
- **D7 (one PR)**: one PR for the issue. All items commit to `feature/pan-5504`.
- **D8 (titles)**: a page is retitled only where its work item says so: `harness-landscape` ("Coding agent harness survey:
  skills, MCP and AGENTS.md"), `prompts` ("Prompt Templates and the renderPrompt Loader"), `state-model` ("Overdeck state
  model: where state lives") and `agents-directory` ("Agents Page and Agents Directory"). Each gets `sidebarTitle` with its
  old title. The prompts research pass marked its retitle optional because an older PRD (`.pan/drafts/PAN-5483.md:470`)
  said "keep". The issue's own rule ("specific searchable title") is newer and authoritative, so the retitle applies.
  Slugs and file names never change.
- **D9 (Vite anchor)**: rename `## Slow Vite/React Frontend with Multiple Workspaces` in `reference/troubleshooting.mdx` to
  `## Slow Vite and React Frontend with Multiple Workspaces` (row V1), whose slug is
  `slow-vite-and-react-frontend-with-multiple-workspaces`. Point the docker-setup link at it (row V2, which replaces
  docker-setup row 22's replacement text). A slash-free slug works the same on the local checker and the live site. The
  only inbound link to the old heading is the broken docker-setup link.
- **D10 (unverifiable claims)**: claims about third-party tools (other harnesses' flags and plugin systems, PostHog,
  Google Stitch, Docker Desktop, WSL) and market figures ("~32 tools read SKILL.md", the legacy-codebases ROI table) stay
  as written unless repo code contradicts them. The appendices list them under "Unverifiable (kept)". The harness pass
  proposed reversing "omp has no MCP" on the strength of one code comment (`src/lib/runtimes/pi-family.ts:52-53`, "its TUI
  wait also holds for MCP servers to finish connecting"). Nothing else in the repo confirms it (`overdeckMcp: false` for
  ohmypi only says Overdeck does not give omp its own server), so the third-party claim stays:
  - **skip** harness-landscape row 8 (keep "holdout (pi and its fork ohmypi/omp).", dash pass only);
  - **skip** row 20 and block R5 (keep the omp "No MCP" lines, dash pass only);
  - apply row 29 with block R10, but write its first line as `4. **MCP is near-universal; pi and ohmypi are the
    exceptions.** Neither has a built-in MCP client.` and keep the rest of R10;
  - row 18 still applies (it changes only the pi line).
  Confirming omp's MCP support is a Follow-up.
- **D11 (quoted UI strings with dashes)**: where a page quotes a UI or CLI string that itself contains an em dash (the
  `Disk low` error, the Agents page `idle — no known blocker` label, the lanes disk-low message), the page paraphrases it
  instead of quoting it, so the dash rule holds. The rows give the paraphrase. Changing the code strings is a Follow-up.
- **D12 (added content)**: the short additions the passes proposed stay in scope because a reader of that page needs them:
  lanes block R1 and workers block W1 (one-line pointers to the MCP lane and worker tools), legacy-codebases block R14
  (codebase map, project rules, memory facts), skill-pack-authoring block R3 (MCP servers), template-conversations block R1
  (what has shipped since the proposal). Each links the page that owns the topic.
- **D13 (ohmypi)**: the `ohmypi` harness is registered, has a policy rule and is in the model picker on the base, so the
  page keeps "Supported (alternative)". Its planned removal is a Follow-up.

## Repo rules that apply

- Work only inside `workspaces/feature-pan-5504` on branch `feature/pan-5504`. Never `git checkout` another branch. Never
  `git stash`.
- One commit per item, with an `Item: <item-id>` trailer, the AC trailers, and the `PR-note:` line. `pan task done` checks
  the trailer on the pushed commit, so push before calling it.
- Docs-only: do not run the full `npm test`. The checks are the dash grep, `npm run lint:docs`, the two named vitest files
  (agent-text-cli-verbs and telemetry-docs), and, in the validation item, `npx -y mint@4.3.49 validate` and
  `npx -y mint@4.3.49 broken-links --check-anchors`.
- While you verify a claim, never send a destructive HTTP request and never run a `pan` command other than
  `pan <verb> --help`.
- Issue and PR references in prose are markdown links (`[#5537](https://github.com/eltmon/overdeck/issues/5537)`).
- Keep every `<placeholder>` inside backticks (MDX safety). Never write the words "bead", "beads" or "bd".

## Common procedure for every page item

0. **Code wins.** If a primary source contradicts a row (the research was wrong, or main moved), skip the row and record
   it in the PR note. Leave that row's acceptance criterion unmet and run `pan task block` with the evidence. Never write a
   claim the code contradicts to satisfy a row.
1. **Apply the change table first.** For each row of the item's range, find the grep anchor with `grep -n -F`, make the
   change, and use the evidence for the wording. Some anchors contain an em dash copied from the page, so apply every row
   before step 4. "Replacement blocks" (R1, W1, S1, …) in the page's appendix hold the exact text for rows that point to
   them. If an anchor no longer matches because another edit moved or reworded it, find the claim by meaning; if the claim
   is gone, skip the row and say so in the PR note. Anchor cells: the anchor is the text between the outer backticks of
   the "Grep anchor" cell (for a cell written with triple backticks, the text between them, trimmed). A `\|` in a cell is a
   plain `|` on the page.
2. **Spot-check before deleting.** Before deleting a whole section, re-run the row's evidence grep once. If the evidence no
   longer holds (main moved), keep the section and list it as a Follow-up.
3. **Residual rule.** Read the whole page (or the item's range) once. For a claim the table does not list that looks
   doubtful, verify it against a primary source and fix the page if the code disagrees. Do not chase claims that look
   right; the appendices' "Verified, keep" lists cover the claims the passes checked.
4. **Dashes.** Remove every en and em dash in the item's range (dash rule), in the same commit. Check that each
   replacement keeps the sentence's meaning.
5. **Frontmatter.** Apply the item's title and description changes, if any. Confirm the description is 50 to 160
   characters and dash-free, and quote any value containing `: `.
6. **Links.** For each internal link, `ls <path>.mdx`, and for an anchor `grep -n "^#.*<heading words>"`, counting
   `docs.json` redirects. Fix any that fail. An anchor that a later chained item creates (prompts `#prompts-outside-this-loader`)
   is checked by the later item.
7. **MDX safety.** Keep any new `<word>` placeholder inside backticks. Run `npm run lint:docs`.
8. **Commit** with `Item: <id>`, its AC trailers and `PR-note: <path>.mdx: <one line>`. Push. Run `pan task done`.

## Planner rows (apply with the named item)

file are chained: `harness-landscape-a` → `-b` → `-c`, `prompts-a` → `-b` → `-c`, and `troubleshooting-a` → `-b` → `-c`.

### WI-Z1 (`validate-and-pr-body`): validate the groups and write the PR body

Runs after every page item.

1. Dash loop over the 18 pages prints nothing:
   `for f in guides/{operating,legacy-codebases,skill-pack-authoring,stitch-integration,docker-setup}.mdx reference/{architecture,settings,god-view,agents-directory,workers,lanes,state-model,harness-landscape,posthog-agents,template-conversations,prompts,troubleshooting,token-spend-report}.mdx; do grep -HnP '[\x{2013}\x{2014}]' "$f"; done`
2. Frontmatter check: each page's `description` is 50 to 160 characters and dash-free; every retitled page has a
   `sidebarTitle`.
3. `npm run lint:docs` exits 0. `npx vitest run src/lib/__tests__/agent-text-cli-verbs.test.ts tests/unit/telemetry-docs.test.ts`
   passes.
4. Run `npx -y mint@4.3.49 validate` and `npx -y mint@4.3.49 broken-links --check-anchors` from the repo root. Both must
   report no error in a `guides/` or `reference/` page. If one fails on a page in scope, fix it in this item's commit. If it
   fails on a page outside scope, do not fix it: list it under Follow-ups and confirm the same failure exists on
   `origin/main`. If `npx` cannot download the CLI, record the exact error.
5. Write the PR body draft into this item's commit message, above the trailer block. An `--allow-empty` commit is fine.
   Sections: `## Summary`; `## Pages` with 18 ticked lines `- [x] <path>.mdx: <PR note>` collected by
   `git log origin/main..HEAD --format=%B | grep '^PR-note:'` (join a chained page's notes on one line); `## Validation`
   with the output of steps 1, 3 and 4; `## Follow-ups` with the list below plus every appendix's "Follow-ups" list.
6. **Post-step after `pan done`** (not an item): append the draft to the PR body with
   `gh pr view <pr> --json body -q .body > <scratch>/body.md`, add the draft, then `gh pr edit <pr> --body-file <scratch>/body.md`.

## Follow-ups found during planning (list in the PR body, do not fix)

The full lists are in each appendix's "Follow-ups" section; WI-Z1 copies them. The headline ones:

- `src/lib/health.ts` keeps the old Deacon ping/kill/respawn loop, but its only live caller is the read-only ping behind
  `pan show <id> --health`.
- `state.json` has no `handoffCount` (always 0 where read); `/api/workspaces/:issueId/clean` has no UI caller.
- Seven prompt templates have no production caller: `review`, `test`, `merge`, `sync-main`, `resume-work`,
  `identity-wake`, `uat-agent`. `work-deft.md` requires an unused `ISSUE_ID_LOWER`. `docs/BUILD.md` prompt wording is stale.
- The "pi is the default for non-Anthropic, non-OpenAI providers" wording is wrong in `ModelPicker.tsx:20`, `CLAUDE.md:51`
  and `configuration/harnesses.mdx:1302`.
- `ohmypi` removal: when it lands, the harness-landscape omp rows need another pass.
- `pan kill <id>` does not stop a worker on another machine; `pan worker list` shows the local stand-in without its issue
  or parent.
- `pan sync --backup-only` runs a full sync when sync backups are off.
- UI and CLI strings with em dashes: the `Disk low` error, the Agents page `idle — no known blocker` label, the lanes
  disk-low message.
- Recapture screenshots: `images/dashboard/god-view-*.png`, `metrics-*.png`, the Health image used by troubleshooting and
  `features/cloister.mdx`, and the Costs image (old brand chrome).
- Confirm whether omp connects MCP servers (`src/lib/runtimes/pi-family.ts:52-53` suggests it does); if so, update the
  harness-landscape omp profile and implication 4.
- The Stitch skill sets `STITCH_PROJECT_ID` while the page's manual setup uses `GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT`.
- `concerns.md` in the codebase map records the one broken anchor on the base; after this PR merges, that sentence is
  stale (the next map refresh drops it).

## Acceptance criteria (map 1:1 to work items)

Every page item meets four criteria:
- its change-table rows are applied, spot-checked by grepping removed anchors and added phrases;
- the dash grep over its range prints nothing (the last item of a chained page checks the whole page);
- the frontmatter passes;
- its commit carries a `PR-note:` line.

The xBRIEF has the specific checks for each item. WI-Z1: lint, the two named tests and both Mintlify commands pass for
`guides/` and `reference/` pages, and the PR body has 18 ticked page lines.

## Non-goals

- No code, test, rule, `docs.json`, `CLAUDE.md`, `README.md` or `changelog.d` change. Code bugs become Follow-ups.
- No page outside the 18 in scope (`vision` belongs to #5499).
- No new pages, no navigation changes, no file renames or slug changes.
- No heading renames or deletions except those listed in NFR-2.
- No screenshots added, replaced or recaptured, and no image files deleted. Only alt text changes and the `<ThemedImage>`
  removals in FR-8.
- No help section added to pages without a troubleshooting or help section.
- No web research; third-party claims stay unless repo code contradicts them (D10).
- No description of intended behavior as current.

## Planning Q&A

Planning ran in `--auto` mode, so no questions went to the operator. These defaults were chosen instead and are recorded
in the xBRIEF's `autoDecisions`:

- **Scope**: 18 pages (17 in the two groups plus template-conversations) instead of the 16 listed (D1).
- **Prerequisite PRs**: both merged; audit on top of them (D2).
- **Help links**: only on pages with a troubleshooting or help section (D3).
- **Screenshots**: delete images of removed UI, keep old-chrome images whose content matches, recaptures as Follow-ups (D5).
- **Retitles**: four generic titles get specific ones with a `sidebarTitle` (D8), including prompts against an older "keep".
- **Vite anchor**: rename the heading to a slash-free slug instead of linking a slash slug (D9).
- **Quoted strings with dashes**: paraphrase instead of quoting (D11).
- **Added content**: keep the short pointer sections the passes proposed (D12).
- **omp MCP**: keep the page's third-party "no MCP" claim; skip the rows that rested on one code comment (D10).
- **Plan size**: one item per page, three chained section items for each of the three pages over 600 lines, and one
  validation item. The plan sets `sizeJustification` because the finalize lint counts each page as its own subsystem,
  while the work is one docs PR over 18 files.

## How to read the appendices

Each appendix is a research pass's output, as the planner verified it. The planner kept the passes' row numbers.
- **Change table**: the rows to apply. A row number with `(A)`, `(B)` or `(C)` belongs to that section item.
- **Replacement blocks**: exact multi-line text for rows that point to them.
- **Verified, keep**: claims already checked, which need no re-research.
- **Unverifiable (kept)** and **Follow-ups**: notes for the PR body; they are not rows.

A "Before:" line in an appendix gives the base figures; the before-state table above is authoritative. Where an appendix
disagrees with a Decision above (for example docker-setup row 22, or a pass calling a choice "optional"), the Decision
wins.

#### Replacement blocks

R1 (row 1, replaces lines 13 to 40; the heading `## PR stuck with stale failed checks after main recovers` stays):

````markdown
A pull request can keep a failed check that ran while `main` was failing.
GitHub doesn't run that check again when `main` recovers, so the pull request
stays blocked even though its own change is fine. Re-run the failed jobs:

```bash
gh run rerun <run-id> --failed
```

Overdeck reads check state from GitHub each time it shows the pull request, so
the new result appears as soon as GitHub reports it.
````

R2 (row 14, replaces lines 458 to 462; the "Consider:" list that follows stays):

```markdown
Run `pan show MIN-123` to read the issue's derived state, the agent's work
history and its health (`pan show MIN-123 --health` prints health and heartbeat
only). If the agent keeps failing on the same work, the task may be too complex.
```

R3 (row 18, replaces lines 583 to 586):

```markdown
The dashboard's **Health** page (experimental: turn on Settings → Experimental →
**Experimental features** to show it) shows host health (CPU and memory), the
Overdeck Anywhere and Deacon status, agent health counts, and reclaimable
sessions. Use it when the running fleet looks unhealthy. A measurement it can't
read shows as unavailable, never as zero.
```

#### Replacement blocks

R1 (replaces page lines 6 to 14):

~~~~markdown
Overdeck builds four of the prompts it sends to agents from versioned
Markdown templates under `src/lib/cloister/prompts/`: the work agent's
kickoff, the Deft work agent's kickoff, the planning agent's discovery prompt
and the model-to-model handoff prompt.

A single loader, [`renderPrompt`](#the-renderprompt-api) at
`src/lib/cloister/prompts.ts`, is the only way code turns one of those
templates into a finished prompt string.

Role definitions are a separate system. They live in `roles/<role>.md`, and
Overdeck reads them without this loader. See
[Prompts outside this loader](#prompts-outside-this-loader).
~~~~

R2 (replaces page lines 62 to 64):

~~~~markdown
Code reads a template under `src/lib/cloister/prompts/` only through
`renderPrompt({ name, vars })`. If you find another read of one of these
files, migrate it to `renderPrompt`. Role files under `roles/` are read
directly by design; see
[Prompts outside this loader](#prompts-outside-this-loader).
~~~~

R3 (replaces page lines 81 to 84):

~~~~markdown
Every variable the template's body uses should appear in the frontmatter as
either `requires` or `optional`. The loader rejects any key in `vars` that
the frontmatter doesn't declare, and any required key that is missing or
`undefined`/`null`. Both errors name the template path and the offending
keys. The loader never scans the body, so a `{{VAR}}` that the frontmatter
leaves out renders as an empty string when the call site doesn't pass it.
~~~~

R4 (replaces the fenced block at page lines 99 to 110):

~~~~markdown
```typescript
import { Effect } from 'effect';
import { renderPrompt } from './prompts.js';

const prompt = await Effect.runPromise(renderPrompt({
  name: 'work',
  vars: {
    ISSUE_ID: 'PAN-742',
    ISSUE_ID_LOWER: 'pan-742',
    WORKSPACE_PATH: '/path/to/workspaces/feature-pan-742',
    BRANCH_NAME: 'feature/pan-742',
    LOCAL: true,
    REMOTE: false,
    // ...plus any optional variables declared in work.md
  },
}));
```
~~~~

R5 (replaces page line 124):

~~~~markdown
`.md` extension. `renderPrompt({ name: 'work' })` loads `work.md`.

`renderPrompt` returns an Effect. Run it with `Effect.runPromise` (as
`buildWorkAgentPrompt` does), with `Effect.runSync` (as `buildHandoffPrompt`
does), or `yield*` it inside `Effect.gen`.
~~~~

R6 (replaces page lines 128 to 130):

~~~~markdown
1. **Load.** The file is read from disk on every call. There is no template
   cache; only the resolved prompts directory is cached.
~~~~

R7 (replaces page lines 145 to 146):

~~~~markdown
Every failure is a `PromptError`, exported from the module. It's an Effect
tagged error (`Data.TaggedError('PromptError')`) and also an `Error`.
`renderPrompt` fails its Effect with it, so `Effect.runPromise` rejects and
`Effect.runSync` throws. Typical messages:
~~~~

R8 (replaces page lines 173 to 180):

~~~~markdown
`resolvePromptsDir()` picks the directory once per process and caches the
answer:

1. `join(__dirname, 'prompts')`, next to the module that holds the loader.
   In dev that's `src/lib/cloister/prompts`. A build copies the templates
   next to each bundle: `dist/dashboard/prompts` for the dashboard server,
   `dist/prompts` and `dist/cli/prompts` for the CLI.
2. If that directory doesn't exist, the resolver finds the package root (it
   strips everything from `/src/` onward when `__dirname` contains `/src/`,
   otherwise it goes up two directories) and tries
   `src/lib/cloister/prompts` under it.
3. If neither exists, it keeps the first path, and the first render fails
   with "Failed to load prompt template".
~~~~

~~~~markdown
---
name: work
description: Primary work-agent prompt that reads the per-issue record, processes feedback, and drives the xBRIEF item loop until pan done.
requires:
  - ISSUE_ID

optional:
  - PROJECT_ROOT

---
Body of the prompt starts here...
```
~~~~

R10 (replaces page lines 227 to 231):

~~~~markdown
`requires` is stricter than you might think: passing an empty string
(`""`) satisfies the check. Only `undefined` and `null` fail. This is
intentional. `buildWorkAgentPrompt` passes an empty `ISSUE_ID` when a
recovery path has no issue id, and the prompt still renders. The caller is
the one who decides whether "empty" is a legitimate value.
~~~~

R11 (replaces page lines 319 to 371; the `## Template catalogue` heading stays above it):

~~~~markdown
The eleven templates under `src/lib/cloister/prompts/`. Four have a
production caller. The other seven still load and some have smoke tests,
but nothing in `src/` renders them today.

### Templates with a caller

| Template | Rendered by | Purpose |
|----------|-------------|---------|
| `work.md` | `buildWorkAgentPrompt` (`src/lib/cloister/work-agent-prompt.ts`) | Work agent kickoff: claim one xBRIEF item, implement it, commit with an `Item:` trailer and push, run `pan task done`, then continue through quality gates and `pan done`. |
| `work-deft.md` | `buildDeftWorkAgentPrompt` (`src/lib/deft/kickoff.ts`), which `buildWorkAgentPrompt` calls for an issue on Deft Directive's pipeline | Hands the issue to Deft's own build, pre-PR and review-cycle skills. Overdeck runs no review or test agents for these issues. |
| `planning.md` | `buildPlanningPrompt` (`src/lib/planning/spawn-planning-session.ts`) | Discovery session: explore the codebase, ask questions through AskUserQuestion, write the PRD draft at `.pan/drafts/<ISSUE>.md`, `.overdeck/continue.json` and the xBRIEF at `.overdeck/spec.vbrief.json`, then run `pan plan finalize` to promote it to `.pan/specs/<ISSUE>.xbrief.json` in the plan home. Never implements code. |
| `handoff-to-work.md` | `buildHandoffPrompt` (`src/lib/cloister/handoff-context.ts`) | Model-to-model handoff: wraps serialized git state, xBRIEF task state, `.overdeck/continue.json` state and summaries with a preamble saying who took over and why. |

### Templates without a caller

| Template | Purpose |
|----------|---------|
| `review.md` | Strict code review, stale-branch check, status reporting through the API. Has smoke tests. |
| `test.md` | Run all suites, compare against a baseline on main, smoke test containers, write the verdict artifact. |
| `merge.md` | Unified merge template. `DO_PUSH`, `DO_BUILD` and `SKIP_DONE_REPORT` toggle between validation-only and full push, build and report flows. Has smoke tests. |
| `uat-agent.md` | Browser-based user acceptance testing. |
| `resume-work.md` | Restart message for a stopped work agent. |
| `sync-main.md` | Resolve merge conflicts when syncing `origin/main` into a workspace branch. |
| `identity-wake.md` | Short identity prompt that tells a newly started specialist its role. |

### Prompts outside this loader

Not every prompt goes through `renderPrompt`:

- **Role definitions** live in `roles/<role>.md`. When a Claude Code agent
  launches with a role file, Overdeck strips its frontmatter and passes the
  body with `--append-system-prompt-file` (`roleSystemPromptInjection` in
  `src/lib/agents/runtime-command.ts`).
- **Review convoy sub-reviewers** (security, correctness, performance,
  requirements) get `roles/review-<subRole>.md` inlined into their spawn
  message by `buildConvoyPrompt` (`src/lib/cloister/review-convoy.ts`), so
  the same text drives every harness.
- **The review and test spawn messages** are built in TypeScript by
  `buildReviewRolePrompt` (`src/lib/cloister/review-agent.ts`) and
  `buildTestRolePrompt` (`src/lib/cloister/test-agent-queue.ts`).

A pull request that changes a `roles/*.md` file needs a commit with a
`Prompt-Change:` trailer (`scripts/check-prompt-change-trailer.sh`).

### Deleted (replaced by the loader)

These files used to live under `prompts/`. They no longer exist:

- `inspect-agent.md` → the inspect verb and its inspect specialist were deleted in PAN-3917
- `merge-agent.md` → replaced by `merge.md` with `DO_PUSH`/`DO_BUILD` flags
- `review-agent.md` → replaced by `review.md`
- `test-agent.md` → replaced by `test.md`
- `work-agent.md` → renamed to `work.md`

If you find references to any of those paths in comments, PRD docs, or old
commits, they are stale.
~~~~

R12 (replaces page lines 383 to 385):

R13 (replaces page lines 398 to 426):

~~~~markdown
### Example: the `work.md` feedback block

```mustache
{{#PENDING_FEEDBACK}}
## Specialist Feedback (ACTION REQUIRED)

Specialist agents have left feedback that you MUST address:

{{PENDING_FEEDBACK}}
...
{{/PENDING_FEEDBACK}}
```

And the call site in `src/lib/cloister/work-agent-prompt.ts`, which lists
the workspace's feedback files and returns an empty string when there are
none:

```typescript
async function readPendingFeedback(workspacePath: string): Promise<string> {
  // ...collect feedbackFilePaths
  if (feedbackFilePaths.length === 0) return '';

  const lines: string[] = [];
  lines.push(`**${feedbackFilePaths.length} feedback file(s):**`);
  // ...one bullet per file, then a "read the latest first" line
  return lines.join('\n');
}

return await Effect.runPromise(renderPrompt({
  name: 'work',
  vars: {
    // ...
    PENDING_FEEDBACK: pendingFeedbackStr,
    // ...
  },
}));
```

When `pendingFeedbackStr` is non-empty, the section renders; when it's
empty, the section disappears entirely. The template stays readable (no
nested conditionals, no formatting logic) and the TypeScript stays honest
about what the block looks like.
~~~~

R14 (replaces page lines 597 to 600):

~~~~markdown
3. **No `prompts/` next to the bundle.** The loader resolves `prompts/`
   relative to its own module directory (`__dirname`), not the working
   directory. If a script bundles the loader somewhere else, check that a
   `prompts/` directory sits next to that bundle.
~~~~

R15 (replaces page lines 602 to 611; the old heading is wrong, so it changes):

~~~~markdown
### A variable renders as an empty string

Mustache renders a variable that isn't in `vars` as an empty string, with no
error. The loader only rejects keys that are passed but undeclared, and
required keys that are missing. It never scans the body, so a `{{VAR}}` that
the frontmatter doesn't list renders empty when the call site doesn't pass
it.

Either add the variable to `requires` (to force the call site to provide
it) or fix the call site to pass it.
~~~~


#### Unverifiable (kept)
- "Why templates at all" history (page lines 22 to 38): prompts used to be built with template literals, `readFileSync`, `String.replace`, and `processIfBlocks`/`processEnvBlocks` helpers. The literal `{{issueId}}` failure and the two drifting merge-prompt builders were the motivation. This is historical and no longer in code. `git grep -n "processIfBlocks"` hits only `docs/prds/active/PAN-408-plan.md:37`, which agrees.
- Mustache.js upstream behavior (partials, lambdas, context fall-through). Behavior used on the page was checked locally with `mustache@4.2.0` (see Verified).
- Principle 5 and the authoring and migration advice (lines 89 to 95, 528 to 549) are guidance, not code claims.

#### Follow-ups (code bugs or out-of-scope doc problems; never fixed by this issue)
- Seven templates have no production caller: `review.md`, `test.md`, `merge.md`, `uat-agent.md`, `resume-work.md`, `sync-main.md`, `identity-wake.md`. `git grep -n "renderPrompt(" -- src ':!*.test.ts'` shows only `work`, `work-deft`, `planning` and `handoff-to-work`; `git grep -n renderPrompt -- packages apps sync-sources evals` finds only `evals/plan-quality.eval.ts:17`, which renders `planning`. They're candidates for deletion, or for wiring back in. `review.md`, `merge.md`, `uat-agent.md`, `test.md` and `planning.md` are still rendered by `prompts.test.ts`, so a deletion must also update those tests.
- `work-deft.md` declares `ISSUE_ID_LOWER` as required, but its body never uses it (`grep -c ISSUE_ID_LOWER src/lib/cloister/prompts/work-deft.md` → 1, the frontmatter line).
- `docs/BUILD.md:161` says missing vars "throw `PromptError`" and BUILD.md lists only the `dist/dashboard/prompts/` copy. BUILD.md:165 says dev mode runs via `tsx`, which contradicts the CLAUDE.md "never tsx" rule. Out of scope (docs/*.md).
- The loader has no tests for the `"optional" must be a list of strings` and `frontmatter must be a YAML mapping` branches (`src/lib/cloister/prompts.ts:96`, `:125`).

#### Replacement blocks

R1 (row 3; goes between the existing `<Note>` and `</Note>` lines):

```
**This is a planning survey, not a support matrix.** Overdeck drives ten
harnesses today: `claude-code` (the default), `pi`, `ohmypi`, `codex`, `acp`,
`kimi-code`, `opencode`, `grok`, `muse` and `prime-agent` (see
[Harnesses](/configuration/harnesses) for the operational guide). Kimi Code
CLI is wired two ways: through the `acp` protocol harness, and natively
through `kimi-code`, which drives Kimi's own terminal UI directly with no ACP
host in between. The other tools on this page are surveyed for evaluation and
aren't selectable. Facts are current as of May 2026 unless a profile says
otherwise and should be re-verified before any decision. Items flagged
**⚠ verify** could not be cleanly confirmed.
```

R2 (row 11):

```
| Harness | Status | Runtime adapter |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `claude-code` | **Supported** (default; built-in harness for Anthropic models) | `src/lib/runtimes/claude-code.ts` (drives the `claude` CLI) |
| `pi` | **Supported** (default for Google, xAI, MiniMax, Z.AI, MiMo, OpenRouter, Nous, DashScope, Groq, Cerebras and Mistral models) | `src/lib/runtimes/pi.ts` (shared pi-family adapter in `ohmypi.ts`) + vendored `packages/pi-extension/` |
| `ohmypi` | **Supported** (alternative) | `src/lib/runtimes/ohmypi.ts` + vendored `packages/ohmypi-extension/` |
| `codex` | **Supported** (default for OpenAI models) | `src/lib/runtimes/codex.ts` |
| `acp` | **Supported** (Kimi only) | `src/lib/runtimes/acp.ts` + vendored `packages/effect-acp/` |
| `kimi-code` | **Supported** (native, Kimi only; default for Kimi models) | `src/lib/runtimes/kimi-code.ts` |
| `opencode` | **Supported** (Go and Zen) | `src/lib/acp/opencode.ts` + shared ACP runtime |
| `grok` | **Supported** (Grok Build sign-in) | `src/lib/acp/grok.ts` + shared ACP runtime |
| `muse` | **Supported** (Meta Muse models only) | `src/lib/runtimes/muse.ts` |
| `prime-agent` | **Supported** (alternative; Anthropic models need API-key auth) | `src/lib/runtimes/prime-agent.ts` |
```

R3 (row 14):

```
Kimi is therefore a supported agent through the `acp` harness, and OpenCode
and Grok Build run on the same ACP transport under their own harness names.
Cursor and Gemini remain candidates: their ACP support reduces the adapter to
provider-specific startup and auth, but they aren't selectable until those
adapters and probes land.
```

R4 (row 19; keep the 4-space indent of the surrounding bullet):

```
    `--skill <path>` CLI flag.** Overdeck doesn't write omp's settings:
    `pan sync` installs its skills into `~/.agents/skills/`, which omp
    discovers.
```

R5 (row 20):

```
  - **MCP**: Overdeck's MCP server stays off for omp. omp's TUI waits for its
    MCP servers to finish connecting before it takes input, so omp no longer
    rejects MCP outright.
```

R6 (row 22; replaces lines 264 to 267, so line 263 `  Codex harness gives GPT-subscription users a first-party OpenAI agent loop` stays above it):

```
  (the GPT-5.6 and GPT-6 models in the OpenAI provider) instead of
  CLIProxy-routing GPT into Claude Code. It's the built-in harness for OpenAI
  models. Work agents run under Overdeck's Codex app-server host by default;
  set `codex.transport: tui` to run them as live TUI sessions under the PTY
  supervisor instead. Neither mode uses one-shot `codex exec`.
```

R7 (row 26):

```
| Harness | OSS / License | Lang | `SKILL.md` | Reads `.claude/skills` | AGENTS.md | MCP | Headless JSON | Native per-session bundle | Overdeck |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Claude Code** | Proprietary | n/a | ✅ | ✅ (own) | Fallback only¹ | ✅ | ✅ | Synth via `--plugin-dir` | **Supported** |
| **Pi** | MIT | TS | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ❌ (by design) | ✅ (RPC) | `--skill` flag | **Supported** |
| **Codex CLI** | Apache-2.0 | Rust | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ native | ✅ (+is a server) | ✅ | Profiles | **Supported** |
| **Gemini CLI** | Apache-2.0 | TS | ✅ | ✅ | Opt-in | ✅ | ✅ | ⭐ `/skills enable` | Candidate |
| **Copilot CLI** | Proprietary | n/a | ✅ | ❌ (`~/.agents`) | ✅ native | ✅ | ❌ ⚠ | `.agent.md` | Candidate |
| **Cursor CLI** | Proprietary | n/a | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Rule scopes | No |
| **Warp / Oz** | ⚠ AGPL-3.0? | Rust | ✅ | ✅ (+all dirs) | ✅ | ✅ (imports) | ✅ | Oz `--profile` | No |
| **Amp** | Proprietary | n/a | ✅ | ❌ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | TS plugins | No |
| **OpenCode** | OSS ⚠ | TS | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ⭐ Custom Agents | **Supported** (Go and Zen, via ACP) |
| **Grok Build** | Proprietary ⚠ | n/a | ✅ | ⚠ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ (ACP) | Plugins | **Supported** via ACP |
| **Goose** | Apache-2.0 | Rust | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ (=ext) | ✅ | ⭐⭐ Recipes | Candidate |
| **Crush** | FSL-1.1-MIT | Go | ✅ | ⚠ | ✅ | ✅ | ⚠ | none | No |
| **Aider** | Apache-2.0 | Python | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | none | No (poor fit) |
| **Cline** | Apache-2.0 | TS | ⚠ | ⚠ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ⭐ rule toggles | No |
| **Continue** | OSS | TS | ⚠ | ⚠ | ⚠ | ✅ | ✅ | ⭐ Continue Agents | No |
```

R8 (row 27):

```
1. **Skill portability is real and already paid for.** Overdeck's skills
   (`sync-sources/skills/`) are `SKILL.md`-format. Codex, Gemini CLI, Cursor,
   OpenCode, Goose, Amp, Warp, pi and ohmypi all read `SKILL.md`, and most read
   `.claude/skills/` directly. Codex, OpenCode, pi and ohmypi already run as
   Overdeck harnesses; adding any of the others is a *runtime adapter*
   problem, not a *skill re-authoring* problem. **Aider is the lone
   exception**: no skills, no MCP, no JSON output; not worth pursuing.
```

R9 (row 28):

```
2. **Codex CLI shipped as a supported harness.** Apache-2.0, native AGENTS.md
   + skills, and a best-in-class headless interface (`codex exec --json`, and
   it can *be* an MCP server). It's the built-in harness for OpenAI models, so
   GPT-subscription users get a first-party OpenAI agent loop instead of
   routing GPT through Claude Code.
```

R10 (row 29):

```
4. **MCP is near-universal; pi is the exception.** pi has no MCP client.
   Overdeck's own MCP server reaches Claude Code, Codex, ACP, OpenCode and
   Grok Build launches, but not pi, ohmypi, Kimi Code, Muse Code or Prime
   Agent, so a feature that assumes MCP must account for those harnesses.
```

- pi "~40 providers", "omp needs Bun", omp install `npm install -g @oh-my-pi/pi-coding-agent` (Overdeck ships no omp install hint: `packages/contracts/src/harness-capabilities.ts:145 "  installHint: null,"`, not a contradiction), omp's `--skill` flag and skill directories, Pi row's "`--skill` flag" in the matrix.

- Claude Code "Overdeck's pipeline prompts assume it" (design rationale; not a checkable behavior).
- Grok Build loading the operator's `~/.grok` plugins/hooks/skills and `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md`.

#### Replacement blocks

R1 (replaces the row 3 anchor line; keep the anchor text, then add a blank line and the new paragraph):

```
`POST /api/lanes/:name/stop` and `POST /api/lanes/:name/reap` with `{ park, keep }`.

A managed conversation can use the `overdeck` MCP server's `lane_start`,
`lane_status`, `lane_wait`, `lane_report` and `lane_stop` tools instead; see
[MCP](/configuration/mcp#agent-only-tools).
```

R2 (replaces the row 6 anchor line):

```
`start`, `stop` and `reap` exit 0 on success and 1 on any refusal.

`wait --run` and `wait --parent` never exit 2: for a stopped lane that never
reported they print `lane <run>/<key> (<name>) is stopped and never reported`
on stderr once and keep waiting.
```

R3 (replaces the row 11 anchor line; two bullets in the "Who may launch what" list):

```
- `--effort` accepts `low`, `medium` or `high` (an `opencode` lane also takes
  opencode's own effort names).
- While the host is CPU-saturated a launch is refused with 429 `cpu-saturated`;
  `--force` launches anyway.
```

R4 (replaces the two lines named in row 14; the result keeps the surrounding sentence intact, so the next page line `not bypass it ... CPU check. A lane that creates a` still follows):

```
refuses with HTTP 507, `code: disk-low`, and refusal text that starts with
`Disk low` and names free, reserved and needed GiB. `--force` does
```

#### Replacement blocks

W1 (replaces the row 2 anchor line; keep it, then add a blank line and the new paragraph):

```
Worker ids are `agent-<issue>-worker-<n>`, for example `agent-pan-123-worker-2`.

Agents with the `overdeck` MCP server can use its `delegate_task`,
`start_worker`, `worker_report` and `list_workers` tools instead; see
[MCP](/configuration/mcp#tools).
```

W2 (replaces the two lines `` `.swarm/` is in the repository's `.gitignore`, so `git add -A` in a workspace `` / `never commits an embedded worktree.`):

```
Overdeck does not add `.swarm/` to your project's ignore files. Add `.swarm/`
to the project's `.gitignore` so `git add -A` in a workspace never stages an
embedded worktree.
```

#### Unverifiable (kept)
- Claude Code Bash tool: `run_in_background: true`, 10-minute foreground limit, background command notifies on exit (line 184 to 187).
- Codex plugin for Claude Code (`codex:codex-rescue` and friends) runs jobs as detached `codex` processes and deletes job records when the parent session ends (lines 277 to 282; the adapter's own comment agrees: `src/dashboard/server/services/codex-plugin-importer.ts:7 "when the parent Claude session ends. So this adapter registers every job the"`).

#### Follow-ups (code bugs or out-of-scope doc problems; never fixed by this issue)
- `pan kill <stand-in id>` cannot stop a remote worker: it finds no `state.json` and no pane and prints `nothing to do` (`src/cli/commands/kill.ts:113-117`); only `--stop-after-report`, the MCP `task_cancel` tool (`src/lib/mcp/tools-delegate.ts:108`) or `POST /api/fleet/workers/:id/stop` reach the owner.
- `pan worker run --harness` refuses `pi`: `src/cli/commands/worker.ts:61 "const HARNESSES: readonly RuntimeName[] = ['claude-code', 'codex', 'acp', 'kimi-code', 'opencode', 'muse', 'prime-agent', 'grok'];"`, although pi is a supported harness (and the default for non-Anthropic, non-OpenAI providers).
- `pan worker list` misreports a stand-in: no `worker.json`, so issue and parent print `-` and `--parent` drops it, and its STATE comes from local liveness, not the remote worker (`src/cli/commands/worker.ts:423`, `:427`; `src/lib/agents/worker/list.ts` reads only `worker.json`).
- External agents: the "done" state is derived only for Claude Code and Codex transcripts (`src/lib/agents/external-registry.ts:360`); Pi, ACP, Kimi Code and Muse transcripts written outside the three transcript roots cannot be shown at all.
- Overdeck never excludes `.swarm/` in a project repository, so `git add -A` in a workspace can stage a worker worktree as an embedded repository; consider writing `.swarm/` to the workspace's `info/exclude`.

#### Replacement blocks

R1 (replaces the diagram and the React/Express paragraph):

```markdown
Overdeck consists of three main components:

- **Dashboard**: a React + Vite single-page app, served with the `/api/*` routes by an Effect HTTP server that runs on Node 22 (port 3011). The frontend gets live state over the `/ws/rpc` WebSocket and agent terminals over `/ws/terminal`.
- **CLI**: the `pan` commands, plus the skills and hooks that `pan sync` installs for each harness.
- **Cloister**: the agent lifecycle manager. It dispatches review, test and merge work, and its Deacon patrol watches running agents (see [Health Monitoring](#health-monitoring-deacon-pattern)).

Agents run in panes of the terminal backend: Herdr by default, or tmux when `terminal.backend: tmux` is set. Each issue gets its own workspace, a git worktree under `workspaces/feature-<issue>/`.
```

R2 (replaces the body of the `~/.overdeck/` tree; keep the opening fence, the `~/.overdeck/` line and the closing fence):

```text
  config.yaml           # Main configuration
  projects.yaml         # Multi-project registry with issue routing
  cloister.toml         # Cloister settings (concurrency, close-out)
  overdeck.db           # Local SQLite database (see the state model)
  project-mappings.json # Linear project → local path mappings (legacy)

  skills/               # Shared skills (SKILL.md format)
  commands/             # Slash commands
  agent-definitions/    # Subagent templates (.md files)
  rules/                # Rules distributed by pan sync
  bin/                  # Hook scripts (synced via pan sync)
    heartbeat-hook      # Activity hook run after each tool call

  agents/               # Per-agent runtime state
    agent-min-123/
      state.json        # Agent state (harness, role, model, status)
      health.json       # Health status
      hook.json         # FPP work queue
      cv.json           # Work history
      mail/             # Incoming messages
      handoffs/         # Handoff prompts (for debugging)

  heartbeats/           # Real-time agent activity
    agent-min-123.json  # Last heartbeat from agent

  logs/                 # Log files
    deacon.log          # Deacon patrol actions
    handoffs.jsonl      # All handoff events (for analytics)

  costs/                # Raw cost logs (JSONL)
  backups/              # Sync backups (newest 10 kept)
  traefik/              # Traefik reverse proxy config
    dynamic/            # Dynamic route configs
    certs/              # TLS certificates
```

R3 (replaces the Agent State Management body; keep the `## Agent State Management` heading):

````markdown
Each agent's state is tracked in `~/.overdeck/agents/{agent-id}/state.json`:

```json
{
  "id": "agent-min-123",
  "issueId": "MIN-123",
  "workspace": "/home/user/projects/myapp/workspaces/feature-min-123",
  "harness": "claude-code",
  "role": "work",
  "model": "claude-opus-5-5",
  "status": "running",
  "startedAt": "2026-11-19T10:00:00.000Z",
  "lastActivity": "2026-11-19T10:30:00.000Z",
  "branch": "feature/min-123",
  "sessionId": "abc123",
  "backend": "herdr",
  "paneId": "w2AD:p3"
}
```

| Field | Description |
|-------|-------------|
| `harness` | Coding-agent harness the agent runs under, such as `claude-code`, `codex` or `pi` |
| `role` | `plan`, `work`, `review`, `test`, `ship`, `flywheel`, `strike`, `sequencer`, `knowledge` or `worker` |
| `model` | Model the agent launched with |
| `status` | `starting`, `running`, `stopped` or `error` |
| `sessionId` | Harness session ID, used to resume the agent |
| `backend`, `paneId` | Terminal backend (`herdr` or `tmux`) and the pane the agent runs in |

**State cleanup:** `pan kill` closes the agent's pane and marks its state `stopped`. Neither `pan kill` nor `pan wipe` deletes `state.json` or transcripts; `pan wipe` prunes only lock files, sockets and cached Codex files from the agent's directory.
````

R4 (replaces the Remote Workspaces body up to the last command line; the closing fence that follows stays):

````markdown
Overdeck supports remote workspace execution via Fly.io Machines. Remote workspaces provide:

- **Cloud-hosted agent environments**: run agents on remote machines without local resource constraints
- **Started like a local workspace**: `pan start <id> --remote` creates the workspace on a Fly machine
- **Automatic hand-off**: when a remote agent finishes, the Deacon patrol hands it to the review pipeline and stops its machine

See [Remote workspaces on Fly](/features/workspaces#remote-workspaces-on-fly) for setup.

Remote workspace management:
```bash
pan admin remote init        # Initialize the Fly.io app for workspace machines
pan admin remote setup       # Install flyctl and configure auth
pan admin remote status      # Check Fly.io connection and machine status
pan admin remote resources   # Show RAM/disk usage across VMs
pan admin remote reap        # Hand finished remote agents to review and stop their machines
````

(Note for the implementer: R4 ends inside the bash block on purpose; the page's existing closing fence after the old `pan admin remote resources` line closes it. `pan start <id> --remote` is inside backticks.)

R5 (replaces the Deacon body; keep the `## Health Monitoring (Deacon Pattern)` heading):

```markdown
Cloister checks running agents every 60 seconds. An agent with no activity for 15 minutes is in *warning*, and one idle for 30 minutes is *stuck*. Cloister nudges both, at most once every 30 minutes per agent. It force-kills a stuck agent only when you set `kill_on_stuck = true` under `[auto_actions]` in `~/.overdeck/cloister.toml`; that is off by default.

The Deacon is Cloister's background patrol. It also runs every 60 seconds, and each pass:

- Nudges a work agent that has been idle for 20 minutes with commits not pushed to its upstream branch, at most once an hour per agent.
- Nudges an agent that stopped on a transient API error to retry, at most once every 5 minutes per agent.
- Compares the terminal backend's live panes with the dashboard, and resumes a work agent whose harness died without a recorded stop.
- Stops agents whose issue has closed, re-dispatches stalled or silent reviews, and hands finished remote agents to review.

Each Deacon action is logged to `~/.overdeck/logs/deacon.log`.
```

R6 (replaces the body of the "Running in Development Mode" code block; keep the fences):

```bash
# Clone and set up
git clone https://github.com/eltmon/overdeck.git
cd overdeck
bun install
npm run build

# Link dev version globally (makes 'pan' use your local build)
npm link

# Start the dashboard with hot reload
pan dev
# → Frontend: http://localhost:3010
# → API: http://localhost:3011
```

R7 (replaces the whole PRD Architecture section):

```markdown
## Planning artifacts

When you plan an issue, Overdeck writes its PRD to `.pan/drafts/<issue>.md` and its xBRIEF plan to `.pan/specs/`, and tracks which plan items are done in `.pan/continues/<issue>.xbrief.json`. These files are committed on the issue's feature branch, so they merge with the PR. A polyrepo project keeps them in its plan home, the repo named by `pan_records.repo` in `projects.yaml`. See [State model](/reference/state-model) for the full layout.
```

R8 (replaces the Developer Skills table rows, the text below them and the dry-run example; keep the `## Developer Skills` heading, the "Overdeck has two types of skills:" line and the table header rows `| Directory | Purpose | Synced When |` and `|-----------|---------|-------------|`):

````markdown
| `sync-sources/skills/` | User-facing skills for all Overdeck users | Always via `pan sync` |
| `sync-sources/dev-skills/` | Developer-only skills for Overdeck contributors | Only in dev mode |

**Dev mode is automatically detected** when `pan` runs from an Overdeck source checkout (one with a `src/` directory, as with `npm link`). Skills in `sync-sources/dev-skills/` are:
- Checked into the repo and version-controlled
- Only synced to developers' machines, not end users

```bash
# Check what will be synced
pan sync --dry-run

# In dev mode the output starts with:
# Developer mode detected - dev-skills will be synced
```
````

#### Follow-ups (code bugs or out-of-scope doc problems; never fixed by this issue)
- In a single-repo project nothing git-ignores `.overdeck/continue.json` or `.overdeck/pipeline.jsonl` (`OVERDECK_PROJECT_GITIGNORE_ENTRIES` lacks them), so they show as untracked; consider adding them (not `.overdeck/` as a whole, which also holds committed memory under `.overdeck/memory/`).
- The "Recovery is trivial" bullet (line 100) is broadly true but a database loss also drops the DB-only rows; left as is since the table now states it.

#### Replacement blocks

R1 (replaces the 3-line top bar paragraph):

```markdown
The top bar shows the clock, events per second, an ECG of events per minute,
**VEL** (stage transitions in the last hour), CPU, MEM and PSI memory-pressure
meters, load, merge queue depth, spend per minute, merges and tokens today,
stale and parked counts, the oldest idle age and the active-agent count.
**? HELP** opens the field guide and the last button toggles fullscreen. A dash
placeholder means the source is unavailable; it does not mean zero.

The right sidebar holds the activity feed, **Flow / hour** (stage transitions
in the last hour, by stage), the **Agents** donut of agents the terminal backend
hosts, and host CPU, MEM and PSI gauges.
```

## Appendix F: guides/docker-setup.mdx, legacy-codebases.mdx, operating.mdx (WI-F1 to WI-F3)

Research pass output, base `574a6be2c29`, rows verified by the planner (anchor unique; `path:line "quote"` evidence greps). Where this appendix conflicts with a Decision in the PRD body, the Decision wins.

Reading the Change column: when a cell is literal replacement text, the outer backticks are delimiters. The replacement is everything between the cell's first and last backtick, verbatim, including leading spaces and any inner backticks. A trailing parenthetical after the closing backtick is a comment, not text. Cells that start with Replace, Delete, Insert, Keep or "see block" are instructions.

### guides/docker-setup.mdx

Before: 352 lines, 0 dash lines, title "Docker & HTTPS Setup", description 134 chars.

Main problems: the page describes an older Traefik layout. Today `pan install` writes `_wildcard.overdeck.localhost.pem` (not `localhost.pem`) into `~/.overdeck/traefik/certs/`, TLS lives in a generated `dynamic/tls.yml` (not in `traefik.yml`), the container image is `traefik:latest` (v3), and no code writes a per-workspace dynamic file. The `workspace.dns` and `workspace.docker` keys are nested under `projects.<key>.workspace` in `~/.overdeck/projects.yaml`, and the unset `sync_method` defaults to `wsl2hosts`. The Vite anchor is broken; the correct Mintlify id keeps the slash.

#### Change table
| # | Grep anchor | Change | Evidence |

| 1 | `- `mkcert` for local certificate generation` | `- `mkcert` for local certificates (`pan install` installs it if it's missing)` | `src/cli/commands/install.ts:350 "execSync('brew install mkcert', { stdio: 'pipe', timeout: 120000 });"`; `src/cli/commands/install.ts:357 "mkcert-v1.4.4-linux-"` |

| 3 | `├── traefik.yml           # Static configuration` | Replace the whole tree code block under `## Traefik Configuration` (from the ``` line above `~/.overdeck/traefik/` through the ``` just after `    └── localhost-key.pem`) with block R2. | `src/lib/traefik.ts:75 "const outputPath = join(TRAEFIK_DYNAMIC_DIR, 'overdeck.yml');"`; `src/lib/traefik.ts:155 "const outputPath = join(TRAEFIK_DYNAMIC_DIR, 'tls.yml');"`; `src/cli/commands/install.ts:376 "const traefikCertFile = join(TRAEFIK_CERTS_DIR, '_wildcard.overdeck.localhost.pem');"`; `sync-sources/templates/traefik/docker-compose.yml:3 "image: traefik:latest"`; `git grep -n "TRAEFIK_DYNAMIC_DIR" -- src ':!*.test.ts'` shows writes only to `overdeck.yml` and `tls.yml` (no per-workspace file) |

#### Replacement blocks

R1 (row 2):

````markdown
```bash
pan install  # Docker network, mkcert, local CA, certificates and ~/.overdeck/traefik/
pan doctor   # Verify setup
```

`pan install` installs mkcert when it's missing (Homebrew on macOS, a download to `~/.local/bin` on Linux), runs `mkcert -install`, creates the `overdeck` Docker network and writes `~/.overdeck/traefik/`. `--minimal` skips Traefik and mkcert, `--skip-mkcert` skips only the certificates, and `--skip-docker` skips the network.
````

R2 (row 3):

````markdown
```
~/.overdeck/traefik/
├── docker-compose.yml    # Traefik container (traefik:latest, named overdeck-traefik)
├── traefik.yml           # Static configuration
├── dynamic/              # File provider configs
│   ├── overdeck.yml      # Dashboard routes
│   └── tls.yml           # Every certificate in certs/
└── certs/                # mkcert certificates
    ├── _wildcard.overdeck.localhost.pem
    └── _wildcard.overdeck.localhost-key.pem
```
````

R3 (row 4):

````markdown
```yaml
api:
  dashboard: true
  insecure: true

entryPoints:
  web:
    address: ":80"
    http:
      redirections:
        entryPoint:
          to: websecure
          scheme: https
  websecure:
    address: ":443"
    http:
      tls: {}

providers:
  file:
    directory: /etc/traefik/dynamic
    watch: true
  docker:
    endpoint: "unix:///var/run/docker.sock"
    exposedByDefault: false
    network: overdeck

log:
  level: INFO
  format: common

accessLog:
  format: common
```

Certificates aren't in this file: Traefik v3 reads them from `dynamic/tls.yml`. `pan install` copies `traefik.yml` and `docker-compose.yml` only when `~/.overdeck/traefik/docker-compose.yml` doesn't exist yet, so your edits to them are kept.
````

R4 (row 5):

```markdown
Overdeck writes two files in `dynamic/`. `overdeck.yml` routes `https://overdeck.localhost` (the `traefik.domain` value in `~/.overdeck/config.toml`) to the dashboard, and `tls.yml` lists every certificate and key pair in `certs/`. `pan install`, `pan up` and `pan dev` regenerate both. Overdeck doesn't write a dynamic file per workspace: route workspace containers with Docker labels, as in the [Docker Compose Template](#docker-compose-template) below. Traefik also picks up any file you add to `dynamic/` yourself.
```

R5 (row 6):

````markdown
`pan install` writes `_wildcard.overdeck.localhost.pem` and its key to `~/.overdeck/traefik/certs/`. The certificate covers `overdeck.localhost`, `*.overdeck.localhost`, `*.localhost`, `localhost`, `127.0.0.1` and `::1`. `pan install`, `pan up` and `pan dev` also create `_wildcard.<domain>.pem` for each project whose `workspace.dns.domain` is set and has no certificate yet, then rewrite `dynamic/tls.yml`. You don't need to run mkcert yourself.

To add a certificate by hand, write the pair into the same folder with the key named `<name>-key.pem`. The next `pan up` adds it to `tls.yml`:

```bash
cd ~/.overdeck/traefik/certs
mkcert -cert-file _wildcard.myapp.test.pem -key-file _wildcard.myapp.test-key.pem myapp.test "*.myapp.test"
```
````

R6 (row 7):

```markdown
Modern browsers resolve `*.localhost` to `127.0.0.1` automatically, so `.localhost` hostnames need no `workspace.dns` block. Without one, templates get `{{DOMAIN}}` set to `localhost`.
```

R7 (row 8):

````markdown
For a custom domain, add a `workspace.dns` block to the project in `~/.overdeck/projects.yaml`:

```yaml
projects:
  myapp:
    workspace:
      dns:
        domain: myapp.test
        entries:
          - "{{FEATURE_FOLDER}}.{{DOMAIN}}"
          - "api-{{FEATURE_FOLDER}}.{{DOMAIN}}"
        sync_method: hosts_file
```

When it creates a workspace, Overdeck adds each entry as a `127.0.0.1` line inside a `# BEGIN overdeck managed entries` block in `/etc/hosts`, and removes it when the workspace is removed. The Overdeck process must be able to write `/etc/hosts`. If it can't, the entry is skipped without an error, so add the lines by hand:

```bash
127.0.0.1 feature-min-123.myapp.test
127.0.0.1 api-feature-min-123.myapp.test
```

`sync_method: dnsmasq` writes `address=/<host>/127.0.0.1` lines to `overdeck.conf` in `/etc/dnsmasq.d` (Homebrew's `etc/dnsmasq.d` on macOS) instead. When `sync_method` is unset, Overdeck uses `wsl2hosts`.
````

Evidence for R7's dnsmasq sentence: `src/lib/dns.ts:168 "const OVERDECK_DNSMASQ_CONF = 'overdeck.conf';"`; `src/lib/dns.ts:181 "const entry = \`address=/${hostname}/${ip}\`;"`; `src/lib/dns.ts:165 "  return '/etc/dnsmasq.d';"`; silent skip: `src/lib/dns.ts:136 "    return false;"` inside `addHostsFileEntry`'s catch, and `src/lib/workspace-manager/create.ts:503 "if (addDnsEntry(dnsMethod, hostname)) {"` adds a step only on success.

R8 (row 10):

```markdown
Overdeck appends each hostname to `~/.wsl2hosts` and starts the Windows scheduled task `OverdeckWsl2HostsSync`, which copies the entries into the Windows hosts file. Register that task once, from an Administrator PowerShell, with the scripts in [`infra/wsl2hosts`](https://github.com/eltmon/overdeck/tree/main/infra/wsl2hosts).
```

R9 (row 14):

````markdown
```bash
cd ~/.overdeck/traefik
docker compose up -d
```

`pan restart --traefik` recreates the container with the current compose file.
````

R10 (row 15):

````markdown
### Docker Compose Template

Point `workspace.docker.compose_template` in `~/.overdeck/projects.yaml` at a template folder in your project. When Overdeck creates a workspace, it renders every `*.template` file in that folder into `<workspace>/.devcontainer/` (replacing placeholders such as `{{FEATURE_FOLDER}}`) and copies the other files as they are. It starts the stack only when asked, for example with `pan workspace create <issueId> --docker`. Re-render with `pan workspace render-devcontainer <featureName>`, or tear down, re-render and restart one stack with `pan workspace rebuild <issueId>`.

```yaml
projects:
  myapp:
    workspace:
      docker:
        compose_template: infra/.devcontainer-template
```
````

```markdown
Still stuck? Ask on the [Overdeck Discord](https://discord.gg/ZpHE2ZTv5P) or file a [GitHub Issue](https://github.com/eltmon/overdeck/issues).
```

#### Unverifiable (kept)
- Docker Desktop (macOS/Windows) or Docker Engine (Linux) as prerequisites.
- Modern browsers resolve `*.localhost` to `127.0.0.1`.
- Docker's IPv4 `0.0.0.0` host address requests no IPv6 `[::]` listener; a port line without a host address publishes on IPv6 too.
- `lsof -i :80` usage, "Common culprits: Apache, nginx".
- "mkcert certs expire after ~1 year".
- "Use Docker volumes for node_modules", `docker system prune`, `docker stats` advice.
- Vite polling every 100ms by default and the 3000ms advice (lives on the troubleshooting page).

#### Follow-ups (code bugs or out-of-scope doc problems; never fixed by this issue)
- `workspace.dns.sync_method` defaults to `wsl2hosts` on every platform at workspace create and remove (`src/lib/workspace-manager/create.ts:499`, `remove.ts:94`), while `pan up` and `pan install` use `detectDnsSyncMethod()` (`src/lib/dns.ts:31-38`, `hosts_file` or `dnsmasq` off WSL). On Linux and macOS an unset `sync_method` writes `~/.wsl2hosts` and tries `powershell.exe`.
- `hosts_file` writes `/etc/hosts` directly with `writeFileSync` and swallows the error (`src/lib/dns.ts:111-134`); as a normal user it silently does nothing.
- `infra/wsl2hosts/sync-wsl2hosts.ps1:6` says `pan doctor --fix` creates the scheduled task; no code creates it (`git grep -n OverdeckWsl2HostsSync -- src` finds only `src/lib/dns.ts:93`, which starts it).
- `sync-sources/templates/traefik/README.md:106` says per-workspace dynamic files "are auto-generated by `pan workspace create` and `pan workspace start`"; nothing writes them. The template `sync-sources/templates/traefik/dynamic/workspace.yml.template` is unused and gets copied into `~/.overdeck/traefik/dynamic/` (only `overdeck.yml.template` is cleaned up, `src/lib/traefik.ts:85`).
- `pan up` prints `Dashboard: https://traefik.<domain>:8080` (`src/cli/index.ts:696`), a host and port pair that mixes the label route and the API port.
- `sync-sources/templates/docker/*` reference templates are not used by any code path.

### guides/legacy-codebases.mdx

Before: 134 lines, 0 dash lines, title "Legacy Codebase Support", description 52 chars.

Main problems: captured skills are not version-controlled by default, because Overdeck gitignores `.claude/skills/` in every project it creates a workspace for; the committed home is `.pan/skills/`. Preferences go in `~/.overdeck/context/global.md`, not `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`. "Cursor, Gemini" are not skill targets. The leadership quotes promise measurements the skill never makes.
