#### Replacement blocks

R12 (row 2):

````markdown
Knowledge Capture writes captured skills to `.claude/skills/project-knowledge/SKILL.md` in the repository you're working in. Overdeck adds `.claude/skills/` to a project's `.gitignore` when it creates a workspace, so that file stays on your machine. To share it, move it to `.pan/skills/project-knowledge/SKILL.md` and commit it. Overdeck copies `.pan/skills/` into each new issue workspace, so when Sarah commits that "we use camelCase columns", every developer's next workspace inherits it. See [Project skills](/features/skills#project-skills).

```
myproject/
├── .pan/skills/
│   └── project-knowledge/     # Git-tracked, shared by the team
│       └── SKILL.md           # "Database uses camelCase, not snake_case"
├── src/
└── ...
```
````

R13 (row 3):

```markdown
Refactor Radar names the patterns that keep confusing AI in your codebase: one entity called `user`, `account` and `member` across layers, a relationship with no foreign key constraint, or callbacks, promises and async/await mixed in one module. It doesn't count errors or measure what they cost.
```

R14 (row 7):

```markdown
## Give agents a map and the rules up front

- **Codebase map.** The `/pan-map` skill writes four orientation files under `.overdeck/context/codebase/`: `architecture.md`, `conventions.md`, `concerns.md` and `stack.md`. Commit them. Planning agents read them first and write them when they're missing; work agents skim them before they start.
- **Project rules.** `pan context edit --layer project` opens `.overdeck/context/project.md`, the committed project layer. See [Context layers](/configuration/context-layers).
- **Memory facts.** Record a rule every agent must follow with `pan memory add`. Overdeck renders the fact index into each managed agent's launch context. See [Memory](/configuration/memory).

```

#### Replacement blocks

R15 (row 9):

```markdown
`pan sync` backs up those folders first and keeps the newest 10 backups. Under `[sync]` in `~/.overdeck/config.toml`, set `backup_before_sync = false` to turn that off, or `backup_keep` to keep a different number. `pan sync --backup-only` makes the backup without syncing, but only while `backup_before_sync` is on: with it off, the flag is ignored and a full sync runs. You can also manage backups directly:
```

#### Follow-ups (code bugs or out-of-scope doc problems; never fixed by this issue)
- `pan sync --backup-only` with `[sync] backup_before_sync = false` falls through into a full sync (`src/cli/commands/sync.ts:317` wraps the only `backupOnly` return at line 337). It also runs the legacy migrations and skill removals before the backup step (`src/cli/commands/sync.ts:286-310`).
- Emergency stop also sets `flywheel.globally_paused` (`src/lib/cloister/service.ts:476`). Nothing reads it (`git grep -n "isFlywheelGloballyPaused" -- src` finds only definitions at `src/lib/overdeck/control-settings.ts:178` and `src/lib/overdeck/merge-sync.ts:47`) and nothing clears it, yet the stop toast says "clear the Deacon / flywheel pause" (`src/dashboard/frontend/src/components/EmergencyStopOverlay.tsx:45`).
- The Disk alert card's Open Resources button (and the push and activity links to `/resources`) lands on Home when experimental features are off (`src/dashboard/frontend/src/App.tsx:410`), so the remediation path is hidden by default.
- `createBackup` skips symlinks (`src/lib/backup.ts:52`), so with the default `strategy: 'symlink'` the sync backup of `~/.claude/skills` omits Overdeck-linked skills.
- `pan restart` and `pan reload` resolve the resume gate differently (`src/cli/commands/restart.ts:593` vs `src/cli/commands/reload.ts:463`): a bare `pan restart` silently re-enables auto-resume on a dashboard started with `--no-resume`.

#### Replacement blocks

R1 (replaces lines 18 to 22):

```markdown
Overdeck mounts skill directories. A `claude-plugin` pack can also declare
remote MCP servers, which apply only after you confirm them (see
[MCP servers](#mcp-servers) below). Nothing else in your repository
reaches the agent: see [What is not
applied](/features/skill-packs#what-is-not-applied) for the full list
(hooks, other MCP servers, commands, agents, context injection, git hooks,
and more).
```

R2 (replaces lines 103 to 111):

```markdown
Overdeck looks for a license in this order: the `plugin.json` `license`
string, trimmed, when one is set; otherwise the first non-blank line of
`LICENSE` at the plugin root, or of `LICENSE.md` when `LICENSE` is absent
or blank. On that line it first looks for a GNU license title, ignoring
case: `GNU AFFERO GENERAL PUBLIC LICENSE` is stored as `AGPL-3.0`,
`GNU LESSER GENERAL PUBLIC LICENSE` as `LGPL`, and
`GNU GENERAL PUBLIC LICENSE` as `GPL`. Otherwise it checks for one of
four case-sensitive tokens, `MIT`, `Apache`, `BSD`, `GPL`, in that order,
and stores only the first match. With no match (including an unmatched
`LICENSE` whose first line names none of them), the `pan skills pack add`
preview shows `License unknown`; it does not fall through to `LICENSE.md`
in that case. `LICENSE.txt` and `COPYING` are not read.
```

R3 (new section, inserted before `## What is copied and what is not`):

```markdown
## MCP servers

Only a `claude-plugin` pack can bring MCP servers. Overdeck reads them from
the `mcpServers` field of `plugin.json` (an object of servers, or a path to
a JSON file with an `mcpServers` key), or from `.mcp.json` at the plugin
root when `plugin.json` has no `mcpServers` field. It applies an entry only
when it has `type: "http"`, a `url` starting `https://`, a name matching
`^[A-Za-z0-9_-]+$` other than `overdeck`, and string header values, with
no `${...}` reference in the url or any header. The preview lists applied
servers on an `MCP servers` row; when any other entry is declared, the
**Not applied** row says `MCP servers`. Applied servers reach Claude Code
and Codex launches only after the operator confirms that preview. See
[Pack MCP servers](/configuration/mcp#pack-mcp-servers) for consent, name
clashes and auth.
```

R4 (replaces lines 118 to 127):

```markdown
Overdeck extracts the trusted commit with `git archive`, so Git LFS content
is never fetched: an LFS file arrives as its small pointer file, and the
preview counts these under **Not applied** as `LFS objects (N)`. Every
symlink, at any depth, is left out of the mount: the mount's copy filter
skips anything `lstat` reports as a symbolic link. Every other file in a
skill directory is copied as is, including hidden files, with no size
limit. Scripts inside a skill directory are copied with it, so the agent
can still run them; Overdeck itself never runs them (except for a runtime
pack like gstack, which is not a layout you can get from a plain
repository or a plugin). See [What is not
applied](/features/skill-packs#what-is-not-applied) for the full table of
things that do not reach the agent: hooks, MCP servers other than a
plugin's remote https servers, commands, agents, context injection, git
hooks, executables, and project-mutating skills.
```

#### Follow-ups
- Pi silently accepts `--pack`/`--skill` and the dialog's Skills choices that it never applies. Consider a warning or refusal for harnesses that apply no packs.
- `git archive` honors `export-ignore` in a pack's committed `.gitattributes`, so such files never reach the extraction. Not documented; a follow-up could confirm and add a line.

## What has shipped since this proposal

Several parts of this proposal now exist under other names. The rest is not built.

| Proposal part | Status |
| --- | --- |
| Per-conversation skill choices | Shipped. **New conversation with options…** has a **Skills** section that turns each non-core skill on or off for one conversation, ahead of issue, project and global settings. See [Skills System](/features/skills). |
| Third-party skills without a global install | Shipped as skill packs: `pan skills pack add` pins a git repository at a commit, and Overdeck mounts the enabled pack skills for one launch. See [Skill Packs](/features/skill-packs). |
| Loading skills into one running conversation | Shipped: `pan skills add <conversation> --pack <id>`, the `add_skills` MCP tool, and **Add skills…** in the conversation menus. `pan handoff --skill` and `--pack` turn skills on for the new conversation only. |
| A named group of skill sources | Partly shipped as pack groups: `skills.pack_groups` in `config.yaml` names a list of packs, and `pan skills set --group <name> on` turns them all on. A group lists whole packs, not single skills. |
| Hiding globally installed skills (open question 1) | Shipped, except for 11 core workflow skills (such as `pan-done` and `pan-task`) that always stay on. Overdeck passes per-launch `skillOverrides` to Claude Code through `--settings`, and writes the same on/off choices into a managed block of Codex's `config.toml`. |
| Harness support | Skill packs load only into Claude Code (`--plugin-dir`) and Codex (a local plugin marketplace). Per-conversation Pi `--skill` loading is not built. |
| `pan bundle`, `pan template`, `skill-bundles/` and `conversation-templates/` YAML, a **Template** selector, `registry` sources resolved through the `skills` CLI | Not built. |
```

#### Replacement blocks

R1:

```
This published report is a point-in-time snapshot. For live spend while agents
and conversations are running, open the dashboard's **Costs** page, titled
**Cost Tracking** (experimental: turn on Settings → Experimental →
**Experimental features** to show it). It shows total cost, event count, budget
warnings, issues over budget, a 30-day cost trend and costs by issue.
```

R2:

```
The report reads one data source, the cost event log at
`~/.overdeck/costs/events.jsonl`, after `pan cost backfill` has rebuilt events
from stored Claude Code, Pi/ohmypi and Codex session transcripts. It covers
April 11 to July 6, 2026. The pre-rebrand era (Feb 2025 to Jun 2026: 734,117
calls, about &#36;54k) was deliberately not imported.
```

R3:

```
| Section | What It Shows |
|---------|---------------|
| **Hero Stats** | Total spend, API calls, tokens, and git commits |
| **Coverage Boundary** | Where each harness's rebuilt data starts: Claude Code, Pi/ohmypi, Codex |
| **Executive Summary** and **Methodology** | Totals, the data source, and the archival boundary |
| **Model Breakdown** | Top 10 models by cost (Claude, GPT, Kimi) |
| **Provider and Pipeline Breakdown** | Cost by provider, cost by session type (implementation, plan, work, flywheel and more), and a monthly spend chart with a coverage note for April and May 2026 |
| **Archive vs Reconstruction** | Why the report no longer claims continuity with the pre-rebrand total |
| **Cache Savings** | Tokens and dollars saved from prompt caching |
| **Cost Reduction Features** | RTK, Caveman and TLDR: what's working and what's not |
| **Top Issues by Spend** | The 15 issues that consumed the most tokens |
| **GitHub Actions CI Cost** | CI runs and their standard-rate equivalent |
| **Codebase Evolution** | File growth, commits by month, and lines of code |
| **Cost-Saving Features** | What Overdeck built to spend less |
| **Codebase Timelapse** | Gource visualization of the git history |
```

R4 (heading kept as a section, image block deleted):

```
## Beyond Cost: the Metrics Page

The dashboard's **Metrics** page, titled **Metrics Dashboard** (experimental:
turn on Settings → Experimental → **Experimental features** to show it), shows
today's cost (UTC), active and stuck agents, and the top agents and issues by
cost. Its handoff count and historical trend charts are placeholders that say
coming soon.
```

R5:

```
The report is regenerated with the `/token-spend-report` developer skill. To update it:

1. Run the skill: `/token-spend-report`
2. It runs `pan cost backfill` as a dry run, then `pan cost backfill --write` when the summary looks sane
3. It aggregates `~/.overdeck/costs/events.jsonl` only, sorted by timestamp
4. It updates `docs/token-spend-report/index.html`, keeping the coverage banner and the note that the pre-rebrand era was not imported
5. It regenerates the videos only when you ask

You don't publish the report by hand. After the change merges to `main`, the GitHub Pages workflow publishes it.

The skill's source is `sync-sources/dev-skills/token-spend-report/SKILL.md`. It's a developer skill, so `pan sync` installs it at `~/.claude/skills/token-spend-report/SKILL.md` only when Overdeck runs from a source checkout.
```

R6:

```
- **Last regenerated:** 2026-07-06
- **Data through:** 2026-07-06
- **Git commits counted:** 6,934
- **Total tracked calls:** 76,462
- **Total spend:** &#36;15,552
```
